--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (R and J) in the given graph, we need to identify all paths between these two nodes and find the cut set that removes all these paths.

By tracing the paths from R to J, we find the following paths:
1. R -> Q -> O -> G -> J
2. R -> Q -> O -> C -> B -> T -> F -> E -> S -> A -> N -> C -> G -> J

To disconnect R and J, we need to remove at least one edge from each of these paths. However, note that simpler paths are subsets of the longer paths. Since we need to disconnect all paths, the smallest number of edges to remove should be targeting the strongest connectors, which in this case are common to paths.

By analyzing the paths, we can see that removing the edges (Q, O) and (C, G) will disconnect all paths between R and J. There are no additional paths to consider once these edges are removed. Other combinations might also work, but these two edges are a minimal cut set.

Thus, the answer is:

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(Q, O), (C, G)].
--- Ground Truth ---

2. **Trace all paths between R and I**: We need to find all possible paths that connect R and I through intermediate nodes and direct edges.

- **Direct Paths**:
  - R → Q → P → G → B → J → H → I

  - R → Q → P → F → E → B → I

  - R → O → C → S → B → I

  - R → Q → P → G → B → I

The minimum number of edges that must be removed is 3. The cut set can be:
